ParlaMint-SI_2017-04-20-SDZ7-Redna-29.ana.u2	Thank you, President. This withdrawal is proposed for two basic reasons. The first is that this law is passed too quickly after this urgent procedure, which has indeed gone into an extreme shortening of deadlines. The first thing is that virtually everything has been going on very untransparent and above all undemocratic since the beginning. The public hearing lasted a few days. The government passed the law in 12 hours. Then came the National Assembly, where the urgent procedure was adopted, the meeting of the mother’s working body was convened before the holidays, the first day after the holidays, and we are now essentially deciding on this law on the first day of the meeting. That's one of the reasons. In short, it is too hasty to adopt this law. The second is that, in fact, it does not even have all the required commercially prescribed ingredients. The first thing is that we are talking about a law dealing with a billion-dollar investment, but it does not contain an analysis of the impact on public finances. There are practically three elements missing. The first is that there is no compensation for the availability to be paid to 2TKK from the State budget. So we don't know how much budget money will go towards financing this project. The second thing is that there is no assessment of all other revenue of the 2DKK on public charges, for example revenue from the tunnel has not been estimated at all, and the estimates of how much a transfer fee would amount to are very variable, between 10 and 18 million. However, we must also be aware that it depends exactly on these new fees or charges, as far as the budget will go. The third thing is that the law sets out 5 types of subsidies that go to 2TKK, and no one knows at this point what other course this 2TKK is actually going to build. There is no investment laboratory, those Members of the KNJF have seen some old laboratories that are said to be out-of-date. On Tuesday, we also heard in Odessa that the construction of a double - line line is also being considered. So a whole bunch of things are still in the air and definitely the law, even a project that doesn't have a closed financial structure, we can't take it that way. The government doesn't even have a plan B, for example, if Hungary demanded something that Slovenia can't agree to. I think that by June 14 – that is to say, by that date, which is the pretext for this law to be adopted, i.e. the European Union's call for proposals – we could have made all these things, and in this way we are adopting a law that is practically a bridging currency for an investment and which is very comparable to what the guarantee law for TES 6 was. We therefore propose that this item withdraw from the agenda.

The draft law was tabled by a group of 79 Members and Members with the first signed by Dr Simono Kustec Lipicer. In order to further explain the bill, I give the floor to the representative of Ms Janji Slug's proposal.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-04-20-SDZ7-Redna-29.ana.u20	Thank you very much. Just at the end of March, we heard a report on the work of the Constitutional Court of the Republic of Slovenia, in which it was pointed out that the end of last year had been disregarded for as many as 10 decisions of the Constitutional Court. The basic reason for the adoption of the electoral law in the National Assembly is to comply with the decisions of the Constitutional Court. Last April, for example, the deadline for the resolution of the discrepancies established by the Constitutional Court regarding the accessibility of voters to persons with disabilities was expired and the deadline for the removal of the legal void, which touched the post-vote, expired already in 2008. Last year, at the consideration of the novel, for which the legislative procedure had already ended on the home working body, where the proposed additions and the articles had not received sufficient, i.e. two-thirds of the support, in the presence of representatives of the National Election Commission of the parliamentary group, invited them to join the drafting of the new amendment of the law and suggested that, in addition to aligning with the decisions of the Constitutional Court, they should also include amendments resulting from their detailed report on the early elections to the National Assembly in 2011. At the level of the Committee on Home Affairs, Public Administration and Local Self-Government, a working group was formed which was composed of representatives of exactly all the members of the Group, and within three months a proposal was drawn up, which is before us today. The Constitutional Court, by a decision relating to the regulation of elections by post, found that both the Law on Elections to the National Assembly and the Law on Local Elections in Discompliance with the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia, because it does not regulate more detailed rules on voting by post. However, the Constitutional Court, in the very sentence of that decision, also referred to the way in which we carried out our decision, which we have taken into account in the formulation of the solutions submitted and transposed into this proposal. However, the Constitutional Court found that the first paragraph of Article 79a of the Law on Elections to the National Assembly was inconsistent with the Constitution, by a decision concerning voters available to persons with disabilities and by voting on those voters with specially adapted voting devices for persons with disabilities. The contested regulation, in the view of the Constitutional Court, does not constitute an appropriate and sufficient adjustment to enable disabled persons to have physical access to voters in public buildings and thus, in most cases, the personal exercise of their electoral rights, and has also raised concerns about the arrangements for the identification of voters allowing to vote with specially adapted ballots and voting machines. The draft law will therefore eliminate both of these anti-constitutionalities. It should be borne in mind that the law in force already provides that each electorate has the right to bring with him a person who helps him to complete or deliver a ballot if he cannot do so on his own account. If the electorate is not available to the disabled, the invalid voter may also vote before the building in which the electorate is located. Election committees also have a voting aid for blind and visually impaired voters, so-called templates. Voters who cannot personally vote for the disease may also vote before the election committee at their home. The latter provision may also apply to persons with disabilities. This will therefore be an additional option by means of the proposed post vote, which will make it significantly easier for the disabled to exercise the right to vote. As I said, some amendments have also been included in the proposal on the basis of a detailed report on the holding of early elections in the 2011 National Assembly. A more appropriate deadline for the submission of applications is proposed instead of the 25th day at the latest 30th day before the date of the vote. The powers of the State Election Commission shall be clearly laid down in the special procedure to determine which candidates have been elected as Members and to whom the right to take over the mandate of a Member at the time of his second function. The provision is deleted that public electoral propaganda must end no later than 24 hours before the day of the vote, as this is already regulated by the Electoral and Referendum Campaign Act. It is proposed that the State Election Commission regulate its business with the Rules of Procedure, following the example of some other authorities, and some minor corrections that do not even need to be listed here. It is certainly very important that the present proposal is based on the constructive cooperation of all the parliamentary groups, for which I thank all the parliamentary groups, of course my own, in this place. There has been a lot of effort, a lot of coordination, but we have a proposal on the table today, and that's what's important. The proposal shall be accompanied by the signatures of 81 Members and Members. And thank you once again to all those involved.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-04-20-SDZ7-Redna-29.ana.u21	The draft law was considered by the Committee on Home Affairs, Public Administration and Local Self-Government as the parent working body. In order to present the report of the Committee, I give the floor to the Chairman of the Magi. Baby Verbich.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-04-20-SDZ7-Redna-29.ana.u22	Thank you for your word, President. Dear Members, Members of Parliament, Secretary of State, very nice greeting! The Committee on Home Affairs, Public Administration and Local Self-Government shall be held at its 34th session. 4. 2017 as a working body, the draft law on amendments and additions to the Law on Elections to the National Assembly was submitted to the National Assembly by a group of Members and Members with the first signed by Dr Simono Kustec Lipicer. In the course of the course of business, the amendments to the draft law were tabled by the Joint Left Group to Articles 9, 10 and 21. In its supplementary explanation of the articles, the representative of the bill's proponents stressed the main solution to the bill's proposal relating to the implementation of the two constitutional principles. The draft law also lays down the competence of the State Election Commission to appoint secretaries of electoral units and district commissions, lays down the grounds and procedures for the discharge of secretaries or deputies, and the conditions to be met by an officer in order to be appointed secretary or alternate to the electoral commission. It also provides that the instructions issued by the National Election Commission are mandatory for electoral authorities. The legal basis for the effective implementation of Article 8 of the 1990 Copenhagen Document is also provided, namely full access to the electoral procedure for observers. At the time of presentation of the written opinion, the representative of the Legislative and Legal Service highlighted two basic legal concerns or questions, all other issues being nomotechnical comments. With the proposal for amendments to the Committee, this point of the Legislative and Legal Service has been removed. The State Secretary of the Ministry of Public Administration said that the Government supports the proposal of the law and firstly welcomes its main objective, namely to comply with the two decisions of the Constitutional Court. The Director of the National Election Commission expressed his support for the bill and said that he also contained some proposals from the State Election Commission that would facilitate its work and, on the other hand, allow new approaches to specific forms of voting. The opinion of the Commission of the Council of State on State arrangements was presented by the Council of State. The Commission fully supported the proposal. In the debate, the representative of the Group of Ambassadors, the United Left, pointed out that the National Assembly should have abolished anti-constitutionality one year ago. Members of the proposed groups stressed that the bill had been discussed longer and wider, the coordination of the Parliamentary Groups and that the President of the State Election Commission had also been included in the debate. Proposals for amendments to the Committee shall be prepared on the basis of comments made by the Legislative and Legal Service. In accordance with Article 28 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee voted on the articles of the bill and adopted them, 16 in favour and none against. In the light of the amendments adopted, the text of the amended legislation is prepared and the amendments adopted are included. The amended bill is an integral part of this report. As Chairman of the Home Committee, I take this opportunity to thank, for the first time, for the response and constructiveness of the coordination meetings of representatives of the parliamentary groups in preparing this proposal for amendments to the Law on Elections in the National Assembly to all parliamentary groups, and for the second time, for the signatures of both coalition partners, Desus and SD, as well as the opposition SDS groups, Novi Slovenija, both members of the national community and members of the MPs. I believe that this bill is just proof that it is the right to change and regulate things for the benefit of individuals and to improve certain processes. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-04-20-SDZ7-Redna-29.ana.u24	Thank you very much, Mr. President, for your word. Ladies and gentlemen, honourable Members! The Government of the Republic of Slovenia adopted its opinion on the proposal for today's Act amending and supplementing the Law on Elections to the National Assembly on 30 March 2017. In its opinion in the first place, the government welcomes the main objective of the draft law, namely the harmonisation of the Law on Elections to the National Assembly with two decisions of the Constitutional Court, namely the 2007 Decision relating to the regulation of elections by post, and the 2014 Decision relating to voters available to persons with disabilities, and the vote in those voters with specially adapted voting devices for persons with disabilities. In 2011, the Government of the Republic of Slovenia adopted and submitted a proposal to the legislative procedure for the amendment of the ZVDZ-C, the main purpose of which was to comply with the Constitutional Court’s decision, but at that time the legislative procedure was unsuccessful. With regard to the implementation of the Constitutional Court decision relating to the regulation of elections by post, the Government notes that the proposal under consideration today follows the solution proposed by the Government in 2011 and is also supported by the Government. With regard to the implementation of the Constitutional Court's decision on the accessibility of persons with disabilities, the drafter fully follows the Constitutional Court's decision, and that is why the Government supports this solution. In the opinion, the Government proposes some minor additions to the specific articles of the bill, in particular in terms of clarity and effective implementation, and also notes that the amendments of the parent committee have been duly taken into account. In view of the above, the Government supports the proposal for a law. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-04-20-SDZ7-Redna-29.ana.u28	The Group of Members and Members from almost all the Members, except Members and Members of the United Left, of the two unrelated Members and of one Member of Desus, submitted to the National Assembly on 3 March 2017 a proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the Law on Elections to the National Assembly with the basic aim of eliminating the found non-constitutionality of the law relating to discrimination against persons with disabilities in respect of the exercise of electoral rights and on irregularities in the voting by post in the existing law. In addition, we have responded to the warnings of the State Election Commission and have included in the paper some of its proposals, which will facilitate its work, simplify certain electoral processes, allow more effective and external control of the elections, and, on the other hand, have allowed new hunger approaches to be put in place to special forms of voting, especially those with disabilities. The abolition of anti-constitution in laws is within the competence of the National Assembly as a legislator and in the past, or even in this mandate, some amendments to the Law on Elections to the National Assembly have already been tabled, with the aim of aligning with the provisions of the Constitutional Court, but these legislative procedures have unfortunately failed. We are therefore pleased in the SMC Group that, through constructive cooperation, we have succeeded in bridging political differences and almost at the level of the entire National Assembly, with the exception of the exceptions mentioned above, to unify ourselves with respect for those provisions issued under Article 48 of the Constitutional Court Act, and thus with respect to respect for the constitutional principles of the rule of law and the division of power. In this regard, it is worth noting that the Constitutional Court in its decisions has repeatedly pointed out that there is a serious violation of those constitutional principles if the legislator does not respond to its decisions. In the amended bill, which we are considering today at the plenary session, we have almost completely eliminated legal concerns or taken into account the comments made by the Law and Legal Service and the Government, as well as some of the comments made by the Municipality of Ljubljana. In the SMC Group, we would like to point out that the introduction of a vote by post for persons with disabilities represents only an additional option and does not constitute a substitute for voting with voting machines, which represent a way of voting personally in the electorate. However, we warn or, according to our assessment, increase the possibility that, with lower costs, the voice will give out significantly more people with disabilities than ever before. The exercise of the right to vote by means of voting devices does not cover as many categories of disability as this new possibility of voting by post. He also expressed the belief that such a solution would pass the proportionality test. However, he regretted that not all persons with disabilities were able to vote in person because of their handicap and therefore all legislation in the Member States of the European Union, including Slovenia, allows for a vote by another person, whether in the electorate or at home or in the post-vote, and that is why, Slovenia is the only exception, it has not decided to use the voting devices which the German Constitutional Court has even banned. In the SMC Group, we will, as active co-founders and co-proposals of the proposed law, of course, support the proposed law, but we would also like the support of the bill to be shown by the few Members and Members who have not joined the amendment as co-proposers. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-04-20-SDZ7-Redna-29.ana.u33	The word is given by the Group of Social Democrats, a magi for her. Bojana Mursich.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-04-20-SDZ7-Redna-29.ana.u34	Thank you very much, President, for your word. Dear Secretary of State, colleagues and colleagues! Members and Members of the Social Democrats are among the 81 signatories to the draft amendments and to the Act on Elections to the National Assembly. We primarily want to regulate the two issues on which the Constitutional Court has established an unconstitutional situation in recent years with decisions. In the first decision, the Constitutional Court found that the law was inadequate and insufficient to deal with the question of the consideration of the votes which had arrived by post. This content is now carefully regulated by the time of arrival of the mail to the electoral commission and the stamps on the envelopes proving the time of delivery of the mail. In the second decision, the Constitutional Court decided that all voters must be accessible to persons with disabilities, which is clearly defined by the legal text, but the issue of accessibility of voters for persons with disabilities is not yet sufficient by law. In the fact that we have more than half of all in Slovenia, that is over a thousand 600 voters, to arrange for them to be accessible to the disabled, then we have much work to do in this area. With an up-to-date cooperation with the National Election Commission, the main work mentioned will be on the District Election Commissions. The latter will probably have to look for new polling sites on one side or propose solutions to make people with disabilities available in the current electorate and, on the other hand, ensure that they do not simply solve this issue by eliminating voters. Social Democrats are aware that this is also a sensitive issue related to financial resources, so it is essential that we assess the need to tackle the issue of accessibility as soon as possible. We are convinced that the primary wish of all disabled persons is to allow them to vote on their mother's ballot. Some other issues that have proved to be unregulated, inadequate or inadequate in the practice of conducting elections and referendums are now being addressed in a more appropriate manner. Thus, the law provides for the abolition of special voting devices intended for disabled persons who have proved themselves to be a very low margin of voting in practice, with the participation of non-disabled persons in voting on these facilities. However, the total costs associated with these voting devices were high. For persons with disabilities, an additional voting option is envisaged by post, which could make it easier for many people to participate or vote in elections. Furthermore, the bill provides that individual members and their deputies and secretaries and deputy secretaries may be relieved of their functions in the event of their non-disclosure in the electoral commission. In doing so, it should be stressed that, unfortunately, in practice, there are still cases where individual members of the electoral commissions do not attend. They do not even justify their failures and thus make the work of the electoral authorities more difficult and delegate their part of the task to other members, which cannot be accepted. An important novelty brought by the law is also that candidate lists will be submitted after a new one no later than 30 days before the day of the vote, which in practice means 5 days earlier than before. In this way we will also gain some day more in sending full votes to our citizens, who will vote by mail from abroad. The draft law also provides an adequate legal basis for the State Election Commission to regulate its business with the Rules of Procedure in more detail and lays down the obligation to comply with the instructions of the State Election Commission to other electoral bodies, in particular the electoral commissions and the District Election Commissions. In doing so, we need to know that the electoral commission is responsible for ensuring the legality of the elections and for the uniform application of the provisions of the law governing electoral procedures. Finally, by means of a proposal for a law, we also establish an appropriate legal basis for the effective implementation of Article 8 of the 1990 Copenhagen Document, which provides full access to the electoral procedure for observers. Members and Members of the Social Democrats are of the opinion that the proposed amendments and additions to the Law on Elections to the National Assembly are good and for the purpose of better implementing electoral procedures in practice are necessary and useful, so we will support them in our parliamentary group. Thank you very much.

The second thing that has disturbed us in the proposal is the abolition of special voting devices. It is true that, with regard to these provisions, the Constitutional Court has not found the contrary to the Constitution, but such a decision has been taken because the law does not specify the conditions and manner in which the electorate is designated and equipped with such an apparatus. In the decision itself, several possible solutions are listed and this proposal does not take into account either of them, because it simply shuts down these voting devices. This measure is seen as abolishing a standard that has already been achieved and therefore the question on the ground is whether the solution is appropriate and whether it is even in line with the Constitutional Court's decision. On the basis of the United Left, we fear that, if possible, we will be re-examination of the provisions of this law at the beginning. We fear that the chances of people with disabilities giving their own votes in elections after this law will not be any better, and given that we have not yet received concrete answers to our questions, there is also the possibility that voters will be imprisoned in the next elections. Although we will not oppose the proposal in the United Left, we will only support it if we obtain the above assurances from the Government and, of course, if our amendments are adopted, we will delete the disputed provisions concerning the resolution of the members of the Commission. ParlaMint-SI_2017-04-20-SDZ7-Redna-29.ana.u38	Honorable President, colleagues and colleagues! The right to vote is the foundation on which the whole parliamentary democracy and democratic structure of the country are based. Without elections, of course, there is no democracy. That is why the issue of electoral law must be viewed with great care and sensitivity, especially when it relates to the situation of persons with disabilities. On this issue, the Constitutional Court has decided three years ago that the Law on Elections to the National Assembly is inconsistent with the Constitution. In the Court’s view, the existing legal regime does not constitute an appropriate and sufficient adjustment to enable disabled persons to have free physical access to voters in public buildings and thus, in most cases, to obstruct the personal exercise of their electoral rights. It is also in line with the Constitutional Court's decision that the Constitution does not comply with the provisions governing the election of voters, which allow for a vote with specially adapted ballots and voting machines, since the law does not provide for obligations and no criteria for determining the eligible voters. Their determination is left to the discretion of the constituencies of the electoral commissions. It is already clear from this arrangement that disabled persons are not guaranteed such flexibility by voters as to ensure their actual equality to the fullest extent possible. In the view of the Constitutional Court, it is therefore an intervention in the right of persons with disabilities to be treated in a non-discriminatory manner in relation to electoral law. The Constitutional Court issued the decision in which it made these observations three years ago. The government has been late for a year with a bill. Also due to the fact that the Law on Elections to the National Assembly represents one of the most important laws to ensure the democratic order of the Republic of Slovenia, it is high time for the National Assembly to abolish the unconstitutional regime of electoral law. The proposal of this law stipulates that voters must be accessible to all persons with disabilities, as well as the amendment of Article 81 by Austrian example, allowing for the voting by post to be done by persons with disabilities. We prepared a personal assistance law in New Slovenia that helps disabled people to improve their lives as much as possible. We are pleased that the National Assembly has adopted our proposal for a law. This bill largely pursues similar objectives related to the assistance of persons with disabilities only by focusing on a slightly more specific area of electoral law. Moreover, the obligation imposed by the Constitutional Court is very clear. In Novi Slovenije we will therefore support the amendment of the Law on Elections to the National Assembly. However, we hope that this will not result in the abolition of certain voters, because then we have not done much to improve active electoral rights.

Now it is added that this unscrupulous function is being performed. In the view of the United Left, this should affect their independence and independence. In some of the positions of the parliamentary groups, it has been explained by what the Director of the State Election Commission has also said that it is a real problem when certain members of their function do not do as they should when they do not attend meetings. This does not mean that things are not only involved during the duration of the elections, but also in the meantime, and this poses a serious problem for the functioning of the committees. The United Left here says that solutions are also a call to political responsibility and that this means an intervention in independence and autonomy, as I said, that they are somehow linked to instructions and that there is no appeal procedure. Here I would point out that the absence of any protection in this law does not mean that there is no possibility of a complaint, but against this decision it is possible to raise a constitutional dispute, as the law provides in the administrative dispute that, if no other legal remedy is provided for in a particular case, the judicial protection of the rights and legal benefits of individuals against decisions and actions of the public authorities is, of course, ensured in an administrative dispute. I would also like to point out that the United Left has always participated in these meetings, in this working group and that, as participants, they also know exactly why and how the solutions to Articles 9 and 10 really appeared and what the purpose of these solutions was. Of course, it must be acknowledged that the original solution envisaged was not appropriate and was unclear and incomplete, so they had comments on those solutions both by the Government and by the Legal and Legislative Service, both of them warned that such solutions could indeed undermine the independence of the members of the electoral bodies, but for this purpose an amendment was drafted by the committee which included a reason for the discharge of the non-disclosure of the function. This reason follows the solution set out in the 2011 Draft Law on Elections to the National Assembly. That novel was not adopted at the time, and its proposer was the Government. And that novelty contained the possibility of dismissing members for reasons of non-participation of a member in the enforcement of the electoral tender act. That's how that diction went down at the time. And this solution, in the view of the working group in which you proposed these amendments, was too narrow and therefore this solution, which is now on the table in this proposal, is an unscrupulous exercise of office, and this unscrupulous exercise of office implies the non-participation of a member at the electoral body meetings, which is not limited to the time of the election, but also to the tasks related to the conduct of the elections, its inactivity in the part of the body itself. With this amendment to the Committee, the thought previously expressed by the Rule of Law and the Government has been overturned. In addition, it should be stressed that such a solution in this proposal now follows somehow the legal logic of our legal order, our legal system, namely that the body that appointed the person in a particular function can, of course, also resolve it under certain conditions, and in particular can resolve it if we are talking about some ground of guilt. Let me also read a piece of the magnetogram from my mother's working body, quote the representative of the Legal and Legislative Service: “We have expressed our first concern about the proposed regulation of the discharge reasons. We have stressed, taking into account the constitutional practice that the discharge reasons can have a significant impact on the independence and impartiality of the electoral commissions, which is necessary, both by the Constitutional Court, because of the equality of electoral law. Therefore, in our opinion, there was a very controversial proposal that these officials should be resolved on the basis of a single proposal, or a request from a parliamentary group or political party, without any substantive reasons." And then the representative of the Legal and Legislative Service continues: "We find that with the amendment of the Committee this thought has been removed." So you're fired. I would also like to draw attention to the legislative material of 1992 when the initial draft Law on Elections to the National Assembly was adopted, and it is clear from the explanation of the bill's proposal that, in my opinion, the electoral authorities are some kind of court-political tribunal concerned with the legality and legitimacy of all electoral processes and that, therefore, representatives of political parties should also be in its composition. This proposal also points out that, of course, the composition must also reflect the relationship between political parties. If I now read from the Rapporteur on 14 May 1992, there is a justification for the proposal to issue the Elections Act to the National Assembly. I quote on the principles on which the law is based: “The organization of elections and the functioning of electoral bodies is based on the following principles: as far as possible, the proportional representation of political parties is taken into account in the composition of the electoral bodies.” And then from the next chapter, the main solutions to the law. Under point 3.4, the last sentence, entitled “In particular, their composition, in which both legal experts and proportionate political parties are represented, is more detailed.” If so, some active control of political parties is expected, but of course this does not mean, as the United Left argues, that this active control allows these members to give instructions. However, it is legitimate for these members to expect to carry out their functions, as I said, actively and conscientiously, and to exercise this control, for which they were also appointed primarily. Therefore, I do not see any difficulty in seeing that if there is an inactive member who does not participate in the work of the electoral authority or goes there alone and only because of the financial compensation he receives and would, of course, continue to perform this function, it is perfectly legitimate, in my view, that such a member is called off or dismissed. As regards the dismissal of members of the lower electoral bodies from judges and legal experts by the State Election Commission, I should point out that the provisions of the current law, which ensure the legality of the elections, and also ensure the uniform application of the provisions of the law relating to electoral procedures, also harmonise the work of electoral commissions, electoral units and district electoral committees and provide some professional guidance on the implementation of the law and oversee their work, which is why these proposed solutions actually fully follow the objectives of the original 1991 law, which I have already read, and somehow follow this established hierarchy among the electoral bodies. This is based precisely on those provisions and shall apply in the same way as in the judicial branch of the Authority. These provisions allow the State Election Commission to exercise this control I spoke about, and without the possibility of releasing the members of the lower electoral authorities for the unlawful reasons of this control or task, the State Election Commission cannot at all carry out this task. It's the only way. As for the secretaries of the electoral authorities and their orders, let me point out that secretaries are not officers. And because they are not officials, they don't make a mercurial decision, but they're some government officials, some public officials who only perform administrative tasks for the electoral commission and care for some support environment, for some support part of these commissions. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-04-20-SDZ7-Redna-29.ana.u60	Thank you so much for saying that. Your Honor! In front of you is the second Slovenian medium-term fiscal framework, more specifically a decree on the framework for the preparation of the budget of the state sector for 2018-2020, which is prepared in accordance with Article 6 of the Fiscal Rule Act. The decree sets out the target deficit and the maximum possible amount of expenditure per year for the country sector as a whole and for the four public treasury finances for the next 3 years. The country's target deficit for 2018 decreases to 0.2% of gross domestic product and in 2019 and 2020 Slovenia will record a surplus of 0.2% and 0.4% of gross domestic product. The adoption of the fiscal framework in the National Assembly represents a key medium-term focus and commitment of Slovenian public financial policy. The framework is based on the spring forecast of the macroeconomic indicators of Umarje, the first assessment by the Statistical Office of RS of the deficit achieved in 2016 and the data of current fiscal flows. Data on the level of last year's deficit and improved economic forecast confirm that Slovenia is higher in the economic cycle than originally expected, while at the same time showing that both the more favourable public financial result and the new economic forecast are due to cyclical effects and government measures. The fiscal framework aims at achieving the medium-term balance of public finances, while more favourable macroeconomic trends nevertheless allow for higher ceilings on the expenditure of the state sector and its individual treasury. This continues to mean that the increase in government revenue is higher than the increase in expenditure. This enables us to progressively achieve the public financial objectives in a way that is partly in line with the rules of the European Union, while at the same time striving to maintain a broad-based stable economic growth, which is no longer based solely on exports, but also on strengthening household demand and domestic investment. In order to address the deficit in line with the requirements of the Stability and Growth Pact, the Government foresees that, with the existing methodology of the European Commission and the accounts of the Ministry of Finance, the balance of public finances, without a negative impact on economic growth, can be achieved by gradually reducing the deficit by the end of 2020. This follows the provisions of Article 148 of the Constitution and the Fiscal Rule Act. This year's framework was also assessed for the first time by the Fiscal Council. This notes that the structural balance is reduced by lower dynamics than the annual requirement for an economy in normal times under European Commission rules. The Fiscal Council therefore expects the Government to prepare a set of additional measures this year in order to achieve sufficient effort in 2016 and 2017 or at least in 2018 and 2019. In addition, it states that expenditure should be limited by reaching a level playing field in 2019. In its position on the assessment of the Fiscal Council, the Government notes that during the period when we approach the medium-term objective, the Fiscal Rule Act does not prescribe the way and formula for the reduction of the structural balance. The achievement of the mid-term objective in 2019 would constitute an extraordinary structural requirement and rapid further consolidation that could threaten economic recovery and have negative consequences for strengthening the potential growth and well-being of the population. Therefore, in 2015 the Government decided to follow a fiscal rule requiring more gradual consolidation. The government has enabled the path to balanced public finances by actively managing debt, by adopting public sector wage policies and by transferring to individuals and households where it has managed to reduce spending growth through the gradual release of austerity measures, as well as by achieving opportunistic savings in areas where, despite increasing costs with appropriate policies, it keeps spending at sustainable levels. It takes into account the above-mentioned Government's view that the proposal for a decree is consistent with the Constitution and the Fiscal Rule Act, and proposes to the National Assembly to adopt it in the proposed form. Given the existing risks, the Government will monitor current developments in public finances and, where appropriate, adapt measures in a timely manner. Thank you very much.

The issue of the resolution of the banking system and its budgetary implications was also mentioned. In the course of the debate, the Minister of Finance replied to the questions raised and provided further clarifications and confirmed that the decree was in accordance with Article 148 of the Constitution. In accordance with Article 148 of the Constitution, the President of the Fiscal Council also confirmed that additional fiscal efforts are needed to ensure that Slovenia does not enter into an unconstitutional state by 2020. In accordance with Rule 128 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee voted on all the articles of the draft decree and adopted them. Since no amendments have been tabled to the draft decree on the working body, the Committee proposes that the draft decree on the framework for the preparation of the budgets of the State sector for the period 2018 to 2020 be adopted in the table. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-04-20-SDZ7-Redna-29.ana.u63	Thank you very much. The following is a presentation of the views of the groups of Members. Ms Urška Ban will present the position of the Group of the Parties of the Modern Centre.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-04-20-SDZ7-Redna-29.ana.u64	Thank you so much again for saying that. On the basis of the Fiscal Rule Act adopted by the National Assembly in 2015, the decision-making process on the key public financial objectives to be pursued in public financial planning begins with the adoption of the decision that we are considering today. The proposed decree defines two key public financial objectives or contains two types of important decisions. Firstly, it sets out the country's target balance as a whole and individual public finances, i.e. the target balance for the state budget, municipal budgets, health and pension funds. In the next step, it also sets out the maximum possible level of expenditure, both for the whole sector of the country and for the amount of expenditure of individual public finances which are pillars in our country and which ensure stability and security for our citizens. The proposed content of the decree or calculations on which the maximum expenditure and target balance are based, both for the whole sector of the country and for all four public treasuryes, is based, shall be based on taking into account the different data relating to the expected revenue and expenditure both in this year and in the next three years. This data is based on the latest spring assessment of macroeconomic circumstances or macroeconomic indicators, as presented in the spring forecast by the RS Office for Macroeconomic Analysis and Development of March this year, further from the data from the Agency of the Republic of Slovenia for Public Records and Services and from the financial plans of the budget users for 2017 and from the Statistical Office of the Republic of Slovenia on developments in public finances. All these figures are therefore based on the calculations the content of which is presented in this Decision. On the basis of these starting points and in accordance with the deadlines set by the Fiscal Rule Act, the Government is now proposing to us for confirmation a decree on the framework for the preparation of the country's budget for the period 2018 to 2020 for the next three years. Although this time in the National Assembly, it is only a second time that we are faced with this kind of decision on the preparation of budgets for the medium term, it is not basically about the content of some new matter in itself. The process whereby the target balance and the maximum possible level of expenditure of the State sector were established was also carried out before the adoption of the Fiscal Rule Act, all since the adoption of the Stability Pact rules. Data on the country's target balance and target expenditure were included each year in the stability programme, which represented the Slovenian medium-term fiscal framework. The Government responsible for the adoption of this fiscal framework was the Government which, after adoption, forwarded the European Commission for further consideration in the framework of the European Semester. However, with the adoption of the Fiscal Rule Act, as I have already mentioned, part of the powers attached to budgetary planning is already being transferred to the National Assembly at this stage. On the basis of Article 6 of the Fiscal Rule Act, the Government is obliged to send a draft framework for the preparation of state budget for the next three years before the final approval of the Stability Programme in 2017 to the National Assembly. And in accordance with the deadlines laid down in the Fiscal Rule Act and taking into account the data already available that affect the future revenue of the State sector and expenditure in this year and the next three years, the Government has forwarded the decree we have on the table. On the basis of April's report by the Statistical Office, which we also took note of at the country's core aggregate meeting of the Finance Committee itself, it was reported that, for 2016, Slovenia recorded a deficit of €609 million or €1.5% of GDP, which is €407 million and 40% less than in 2015. I have just noticed today that, after the first audit in 2016, the country's deficit was slightly higher, 733 million euros, or 1.8% of GDP, which is 124 million euros or 0.3 percentage points higher than we were aware of this assessment on the Committee itself, so I am already asking the proposer whether and how this affects the objectives set out in the decision for the next three years. To a certain extent, this better result achieved in 2016, as already mentioned by the Secretary of State itself, is due to the improvement in the state's economic cycle situation and also to the structural improvements made in previous years. In this way, the 2015 deficit improved by 1.2% of GDP and thus successfully addressed public financial risks. This improved result last year, and further calls for additional fiscal effort already this year, is said for 2017, because when we adopted the 2016 budget, we planned a country's deficit of 1.3% of GDP. According to new projections, those that have been available to us on the Committee, including the stability programme annexed to this decree, are expected to reach a target deficit of 0.8% of GDP this year. In the coming years, the decree foresees a further gradual consolidation of public finances by reducing the country's nominal balance in 2018, the deficit will be 0.2% of GDP, and in the next two years we will record a surplus of 0.2% of GDP in 2019 and 0.4% of GDP in 2020. We can certainly see that this is a significant shift towards overcoming the deficit of the sector of the country that Slovenia will eliminate for the first time since EU entry in 2019 and even create a surplus. On this path, we will also record a significant improvement in the primary balance of the sector of the country, which has been positive since 2015. This paves the way for the medium-term balance of public finances by 2020. However, the nominal reduction in the deficit of the sector is not fully reflected in the improved structural balance of the sector of the country in which we eliminate the impact of the economic cycle and the single event. According to data from the draft stability programme in 2016, the structural balance improved by 0.8% and an improvement of 0.3% is planned for 2017. This means that nominal improvement is not largely due to the adjustment of the structure of public expenditure, but also to the influence of cyclical factors. Therefore, the implementation of the established permanent structural measures, which will improve the structure of public finances and investments that will enhance potential growth, is crucial during this period. These measures are foreseen in the National Reform Programme and also in the Stability Programme and are divided into short-term measures, long-term measures and measures to strengthen growth potential. Taking into account and implementing the measures envisaged by both programmes, both the National Reform Programme and the Stability Programme, in our view, the Government is following the road map so that the structural balance of the sector will be balanced by the end of 2020 when the medium-term objective is reached. This will be achieved in a way that will make the growth of public financial revenues higher than the growth of public financial expenditure. It is also clear from the stability programme projections that the overall expenditure of the sector will grow slower than nominal GDP by the end of the programming period. This will reduce expenditure from 45 % of gross domestic product in 2016 to 42 % of gross domestic product in 2020, the lowest level since entry into the European Union. This enables the government to progressively achieve its public financial objectives by striving to maintain, as has already been said today, broad-based economic growth, which will no longer be based solely on exports, but also on strengthening the demand for households and domestic investments. However, the fact that we are still in a transitional period of convergence with the medium-term fiscal objective, as set out in the Fiscal Rule Act, must be taken into account when this decision is being considered. At the time of this law, the Fiscal Rule Act is said, we have discussed a lot that the law sets a medium-term fiscal target slightly differently than the medium-term fiscal objective set under the European Stability and Development Pact, as this requires a structural surplus of 0.25% of GDP to be achieved by the end of 2020. Our rule therefore focuses only on the structural effort rule in the coming period and does not highlight the other two European rules relating to the quality of expenditure and the debt rule. Some warnings and recommendations have been given to us in this context at the Committee on Finance and Monetary Policy itself, according to the adjustment of our public finances to the medium-term fiscal objective, for the first time this year, the Fiscal Council, which we appointed shortly ago in the previous plenary session of the National Assembly, is also included for the first time in some way in the budget planning process since this stage. As an independent professional institution, we have entrusted him with the task of defining, depending on the economic cycle phase for each financial year, the extent of public spending. It is therefore the task of the Fiscal Council to determine in advance whether the envisaged level of public expenditure is appropriate in relation to the stages of the economic cycle in which the State is located and whether it complies with the provisions of the fiscal rule. In an opinion presented to us by the President of the Fiscal Council, she estimates that we are dealing in Slovenia with favourable movements and with a conjuncture in which we could say that our country is already in the normal period of the economic cycle. However, the President of the Fiscal Council warned and recommended to the Government that the fiscal effort in the structural balance should be increased by 2 per cent of GDP in 2018 and 2019, so that by the end of 2017 the government will draw up appropriate measures to actually balance the budget by the beginning of 2020. In this way, according to the Fiscal Council, in 2020, when the medium-term period ends, we will not be in conflict with the requirements set out in the Constitution and the Fiscal Rule Act. On the basis of the Fiscal Council's recommendations, we expect the Government to assess that there is no need for a change of decision at this time to closely monitor the public finances situation and timely action if certain risks are to be increased. We also expect the Government to actually implement the measures envisaged in the National Reform Programme and the Stability Programme in order to ensure the necessary fiscal effort. In the end, if I take it from you. In our current stability programmes, we have committed ourselves to achieving the medium-term fiscal objective in 2020: our medium-term objective is to achieve a structural surplus of 0.25% of GDP, as I said in 2020. This objective, in our view, follows today's decision on the preparation of the country's budget for the next three-year period, where, in view of the more favourable macroeconomic circumstances, the aim of the adjustment rate is also pursued, as I have already said in the introduction. Therefore, in the Group of the Parties to the Modern Centre, we believe that the proposed decree is in line with the current regulation on fiscal policy, which is laid down on the one hand by the Constitution and adopted by the Fiscal Rule Act, and on the other, by European rules. On the basis of the resolution and the draft stability programme annexed to it, we assess that in the future fiscal policy and road planning for final fiscal consolidation will be geared towards supporting stable economic growth and thus ensuring greater prosperity for all of us, and we will therefore support the proposed decree in our Group of Members. Thank you.

However, the other important information contained in this Decision is indeed encouraging. The government decreed that the state budget could spend 9.7 billion euros in 2019 instead of 9.4 billion. The government has therefore found that in 2019 we can spend more than 281 million euros last year without violating the fiscal rule or European rules. In other words, it follows from the decree that Slovenia can provide the missing EUR 200 million of capital for the Divač-Koper route on its own without having to make an additional commitment. Therefore, the EUR 200 million now sought by Hungary for investment in the second indent can be provided by the State without having to make any additional commitment or in breach of European rules. The economic situation, and this is the reason, is only significantly better than indicated in February 2015 when the Government decided that the second track should be built with the participation of foreign capital. And the economic situation is also significantly better than it showed last autumn when the coalition adopted the current decision to draw up the budgets of the state sector. Therefore, this decree proves that Slovenia can provide capital for the second indent without additional indebtedness and without violating European rules. In this way, Slovenia can avoid the demands of Hungary for tied operations. In this way, we can prevent attempts to privatise Port Koper or grant concessions in the port of Kore. And in this way, we can avoid an absurd situation in order for our state-owned companies to subsidise a foreign country with new taxes. In summary, last July the Minister of Restoration answered the question of the journalist Mladina, who said why is the country deciding on a more expensive way to build a second track?, he replied, quote, “just because we don’t have money”. This decree proves that it doesn't hold. We have enough money to build a new route in a way that is proven to be the cheapest in the long term and that will maintain the key infrastructure in Slovenian management. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-04-20-SDZ7-Redna-29.ana.u78	Thank you so much for saying that. Your Honor! I would like to respond to a few of the references in these introductory presentations. It is true that we are today adopting regulations for 2018, 2019 and 2020, but it is also true that the figures that are the basis for all the calculations on which we make these decisions are changing. Today, as the president of the Committee on Finance and Monetary Policy has pointed out, an audit of the deficit published by the Statistical Office has been published. Thus, the deficit in 2015, which was published two weeks ago at 2.6% of gross domestic product, has now been corrected to 2.9. And last year's deficit, which is expected to amount to 1.5% of gross domestic product, has now been corrected to 1.8%. The adjustments are due to the incorrect application of the interest adjustments on foreign exchange swaps, which protected the Republic of Slovenia against exchange risk when committed in the dollar market. The Eurostat methodology requires interest to be recorded as if there was no replacement and that in the event of a euro depreciation that occurred when we have a fixed rate and pay coupons after that rate, the methodology requires that the market rate be taken into account on the day of the coupon payment. As the dollar against the euro grew during this period, the statistical method now shows us a different picture than it was two weeks ago. Specifically, this now means that the structural effort in 2016 will be slightly lower, and since we will insist on the road map, it is said that the numbers will now not change further, this means that we will maintain this path and that the effort in 2017 will be slightly higher. Let me remind you, let's say it's all about pre-assessments, it's called ex ante evaluation. Let me remind you that we planned a 2.2 deficit last year and that the European Commission assessed it 2.4. Now it's a result that's no longer ex ante, but it's ex post, 1.8. I would like to point out that the road or, above all, the speed of consolidation is important. I would like to respond to the claim that we are not in conformity with the Constitution or the implementing fiscal rule law. Article 15 of the Fiscal Rule Act lays down transitional provisions, as we approach our medium-term fiscal objective, which is a net balance, and according to the methodology or rules of the European Commission, a surplus of 0.25% of GDP is required from us, i.e. during this transitional period when we approach this medium-term objective, we need to be consistent with structural effort. With regard to structural effort, however, you can agree in full or in part. With these numbers we now have, we will achieve partial compliance in terms of structural effort, without touching the other two criteria. This rule is long and expanded benchmark, it's a rule of expenditure. Therefore, the Government considers that we are partially in line with the Constitution and the Fiscal Rule Act. As regards what is included in the existing framework in accordance with the rules of enhanced fiscal management, we can plan expenditure where legislation is prepared to such an extent that expenditure dynamics are sufficiently defined, in this context the planned changes in the health system take into account that part which does not require a major systemic change, but only the reallocation of expenditure between the cash registers, is a gradual transfer of non-health services from healthcare to the national budget. As already explained in the Committee on Finance and Monetary Policy, the State budget in 2019 and 2020, unlike other cash registers and from the general balance of the sector, is showing a deficit precisely on the basis of the fact that it will provide additional transfers to the pension fund in order to carry out indexations in accordance with system law and for the health treasury due to the already mentioned transfer of non-health services. Thank you.

It is agreed that in the coming years the State budget will gradually take over non-health services or activities paid so far by the ZZZS, i.e. health treasury, so that one part of the major expenditure of the state budget will be due to this, the other part, to the deficit of the state budget, due to higher payments to the pension fund for the reasons already mentioned. With regard to the health register itself, I would like to explain that the ceiling on expenditure is increasing because the simple labour market is strengthened because more employees, more employees means higher contributions to the health treasury and the health treasury on the expenditure side are clear needs, so we do not choke this cash market for unnecessary and so much as extra revenue, for so long as we can raise the expenditure side that then the balance is still nothing. So it's a flat-rate balance. With regard to what is included in the fiscal risk framework or not, the Ministry of Finance maintains a register of fiscal risks, including potential actions. Of course, we do not take these amounts into account in individual years, but, as we have said here, at the time of the adoption of the 2017 budgets, 2018 we planned slightly higher spending rights, so in 2017, 2018 we have exactly this purpose of potential actions and the judgment of reserved spending rights. And now this rhetoric about compliance, non-compliance, partial compliance and so on. This is the rhetoric of the European Commission. The European Commission has a matrix where countries are allocated depending on whether they are in good or in bad times, because then it depends on what consolidation requirements are. In view of the extent or manner in which States meet the requirements, they are classified as “fullly compliant”, it is said that they are fully consistent, in the “roadly compliant”, they appear to be partially consistent, and in those who are “at risk of a significant deviation”, they are said to be at risk of a greater deviation. In particular, I would say that if we wanted to be “fullly compliant”, it is said to be fully consistent, this would mean 0.6 structural efforts for our country at this time each year. That would be full compliance. It's full of, I think, 8 countries in the European Union. The second category, “broadly compliant”, is said to be partially consistent, means a concrete 0.3 average of structural effort in two years. In order to be exposed to the risk of a greater deviation, all that is less means is 0.2 or less fiscal effort. We have used the rhetoric of the European Commission, and we are partly in line with their rules, which means that we are in line with what we wrote in the Fiscal Rule Act for a transitional period until we reach the medium-term fiscal objective. We take into account the European Commission's methodology, but we do not agree if I return to the beginning, with their assessments of what is the potential growth in this country. Here they have significantly lower grades than we have. How will others be affected, says how it is taken into account in the decree? The law on the second track says that there will be funding when the first European funds are received, it is said that when the first European millions are acquired, so these numbers, we have calculations, we have scenarios, but these numbers because of the uncertain time dynamics in this decree we could not take into account. At the end of this intervention, however, I would like to say that we should just look at the figures on what has been happening to the balance of our country's sector in recent years. From -15% of gross domestic product in 2013, over -5.4 in 2014, we came out of the surplus deficit procedure in 2015, it is said that for the first time in years we fell below 3%, to 2.9. For last year, it is now estimated that we were at -1.8, this year we estimate that we are at -8 so that the consolidation path is the right one, is obvious and we expect it to be sustainable. Thank you very much.

In this case, probably in connection with the Stability and Growth Pact, it can be partly consistent, incompatible, full-fledged and so on. But you cannot transfer this to the legal order of Slovenia when you say that something is in accordance with the law or the constitution. You can't. Since even if something is consistent or partially consistent with the Stability Pact, it does not automatically imply that it is compatible with the Constitution. You can talk about economic terms, economic words and so on, and I can agree with that, but use this in relation to European regulations, not in relation to the Constitution. The Constitutional Court does not know partial compliance with the rules. It's consistent or incompatible. The dictionary of the Slovenian book language does not know partial conformity. Consistency or non-conformity. It's a simple matter. The invention of some new finches can be very quick to mislead the public if the person is not precise. The Czech Republic, because it is partly in line with European rules, does not mean that it is partially in conformity or that it is even in compliance with the rules in Slovenia. This is the inaccuracies, impreciseness, which may also mean some misunderstanding of those words. So that's all I'd give as a replica to be precise about these things, because they mean a lot.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-04-20-SDZ7-Redna-29.ana.u102	Dear President, honourable Members, honourable Members! The second track of the Divača-Koper railway connection is our largest and internationally important infrastructure project. A project moved by the government of Dr. Mir Cerar after 20 years from a dead spot. Today we have a legal building permit, which means we're starting the construction of a second track this year. We have a clear financial structure. We have set up a project company, 2TKK, which will implement the project. In early February, we signed up for a call for EU funding from the CEF mechanism, and in July we are applying for a call for a combination of European funds with other sources of funding. We have a letter of support from Poland, Hungary, the Czech Republic, Slovakia. In its letter of support, Hungary also expressed its intention to invest capital in project company 2TKK. Ladies and gentlemen, I hope to say that today all of us in this Chamber agree that the time has come for us to start building a second track. It is precisely the proposal for a law on the construction, management and management of the second track of the Divač-Koper railway connection, which has thus received a green light on the Infrastructure Committee and is ahead of you today, that will make it possible – to build a second track. The aim of the law is to ensure an effective financing model for the construction of the second track and its implementation as quickly as possible, thereby enhancing the competitiveness of the port, logistics and the entire Slovenian economy. The law becomes the railway infrastructure in our hands. The project will be under the supervision of the Ministry, the Government, the Court of Auditors and the Financial Administration of the Republic of Slovenia. Our goal is transparency. So what's the law governing? Management and management of the second track. The project company 2TKK will be awarded a concession for the construction and management of the second track for a period of 45 years. All tasks will be managed by 2TKK as an investor on its own behalf and on its own account. The built infrastructure will be part of the public rail network and will become state-owned after the end of the concession period. The mode of funding is clear and at least burdens taxpayers. The budgetary financing of the Republic of Slovenia is limited by a capital injection of up to EUR 200 million also due to the adopted fiscal rule and the requirement to reduce public debt. Therefore, the Government has also decided to use the innovative financial instruments introduced by Juncker’s investment programme to finance the project, which is the so-called EFSI mechanism. The law proposes the participation of partnered outlying countries, so we are talking about a public-private partnership. In addition to the capital injections and European grants, the third component of the project financing will be the European Investment Bank loan with a guarantee of the European Fund for Strategic Investments. The law also provides for the inflows of 2TKK during the implementation of the concession. Finally, let me stress that the second track project will benefit both the Slovenian economy and the economies of the Western countries, which are connected to the port of Koper and represent 70% of the transhipment in our port. The growth and competitiveness of the port of Koper and all logistics activities will continue. And what we sometimes do is a little bit of emphasis — passengers will be driving on the second track. Today's adoption of the law is one of the important milestones on our path or even better on the course of Divac-Koper. This will allow us to apply for an already open European tender in July, since 2TKK will become an investor in construction and we will be able to start with the preparatory works this year. Your Honor! I'm counting on your support for the marriage, which is how the first train, thanks to you, will be able to take over in 2025. Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-04-20-SDZ7-Redna-29.ana.u104	President, thank you for your word. Nice greetings to Members and Members of Parliament and also to the Minister with the team! Consider the proposal for a law on the construction, management and management of the second track of the Divač-Koper railway line submitted by the Government to the National Assembly for consideration following an urgent procedure. It follows from the assessment of the financial implications of the draft law that all the loans hired and paid back to project co-investors should be repaid after the concession period. In view of the fact that 2TKK is provided with a permanent source of funding, it pointed out that it would indicate where the operating risks of this company appear. Mr Verbic, the representative of the Council of State, stressed that the Commission does not support the bill. Among the key reasons for non-support, he highlighted a short-term public debate, which took place only a few days before the adoption of the bill. It further highlighted the uncertainty of funding sources for the construction of the second track, with only EUR 200 million in budgetary resources being provided. The representative of 2TKK Methods of Dragonja explained in relation to the legislative service's comment that the most important risk for the project company will be the construction on the second track. This is a very demanding engineering facility, which is carried out mostly after a very difficult karst terrain. The budgetary values of the project are therefore one of the key risks also addressed by the European Investment Bank. The risks on the construction side are linked to the risks of providing funding for the project and possible overruns. Risks are spread over the entire construction period and continue during the period of operation of the newly built infrastructure for as long as the concession ratio persists. In the debate, the United Left Group expressed opposition to the possibility of ownership of other countries entering 2TKK. In its opinion, by preventing this possibility, it is ensured that the route of Divac-Koper will remain in 100 % ownership of the Republic of Slovenia during the 65-year period of validity of the concession. In addition, in their view, the financing of the project is cheap. The investment laboratory foresees that the payments made by Luke Koper, Darsa and Slovenian Railways and transfers from the state budget to the owners of 2TKK would provide a return of 4,5 %, almost half of this input would have to be provided to the Republic of Hungary, meaning that the capital injection of EUR 200 million would ultimately pay substantially more than the long-term loan. On the other hand, other opposition Members have raised a dilemma as to whether we need a law and a company to build a second track. They pointed out that the dilemmas, or the construction of the second track, were not, but the way in which the project was carried out was being questioned. In their opinion, there are companies in Slovenia that are qualified to manage and implement such projects. On the possibility of other countries entering the project, however, they expressed the view that each serious foreign partner's approach to the project was welcome. Members of the Modern Centre Party expressed their support for the bill and pointed out that after many years of discussions on the construction of the second track, the Government had decided to carry out a second track project. In their view, the bill provides a good basis for all further activities, including negotiations with European institutions, banks and the latter. In the view of Members of the Modern Centre Party, it would be unwise for the other countries to completely reject the possibility of accession to the project, their support for the project is still desirable, given that all these countries are also gravitating to other ports in the northern Adriatic, thereby representing direct competition for the ports of Kohorsko. After discussion, the Committee adopted several of its amendments, but it did not accept or become irrelevant the amendments of the Joint Left Group. In accordance with Rule 128 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee then voted on and adopted all the articles of the bill together. Thank you very much.

We have heard today that this does not hold that this is not a condition for the adoption of this law. The project is not sufficiently complete, innovative and does not meet all the tendering conditions for EU funds, so this is an overkill in our opinion. From the statements made by the State Secretary at the Ministry of Infrastructure, Mr. Lebn, in Tarč's broadcast on RTV Slovenia 13. 4. it turns out that the Government is studying the possibility of building a double track, so we are asking in our parliamentary group whether we even know what we are going to build. We believe that the construction of two tracks is necessary, not only what is determined by the project, for which we also have a building permit number in the law. That means we already have something to build. It would be right for the government to ask again whether it was two tracks or just one. With regard to the strategy for the development of transport infrastructure in Slovenia, we should follow this strategy and view railway infrastructure as a whole, not just those 27 kilometres of road. The Second Tier Act brings a new levy on Luca Koper. This is the freight charge that Luka will pay – this will reduce Luke’s competitiveness, the toll premium, which is what we will charge tolls on the highway. The carriers also point out that the increase in tolls is out of the question and that Dars has to repay more than 2 billion credits and can't keep up the toll indefinitely. In New Slovenia we support the development of Slovenia and economic growth, but it is necessary to have good projects that are profitable and bring about better development. We support the construction of the second track, but we believe that we can take another year and prepare the project well or consider another option.
