ParlaMint-SI_2017-04-20-SDZ7-Redna-29.ana.u118	Dear President, Honourable Minister, Fellow Members and Fellow Members! Today, after more than a decade of different studies and putting the second track into space, we are talking about another important step on the way to realizing this strategically important and constructionly demanding infrastructure project. This is an important step after a building permit was issued for him last year. The draft law, which is on our tables, lays down the conditions and modalities for the implementation of the second track, the management and management of the second track as a public railway infrastructure, the role and tasks of 2TKK d. o. o., its authorities and the revenue to be paid by the company. The law provides that the founder and only member of this company is the Republic of Slovenia. However, it allows for the possibility of a business share in a company under the conditions laid down in the Interstate Agreement ratified by this National Assembly to be acquired by another Member State or Member State of the European Union and their share cannot be achieved or exceeded by the share of the Republic of Slovenia, thereby ensuring that the Republic of Slovenia has a majority share in the company and associated rights. In the Group of the Parties of the Modern Centre, we join the Government's position to reduce, on the one hand, the financial risks of the participants in the project and, on the other hand, to the nature of a regional or broader European project, thereby increasing its ability to obtain funds from EU tenders. We adopt the law as a matter of urgency, as this will enable cooperation for obtaining European grants and the CEF invitation to tender in July 2017, which covers a billion euro in non-refundable terms, in which the construction of a second track can also be financed in a significant proportion. In the SMC Group, we believe that such opportunities must be taken advantage of and prepared to ensure that Slovenia succeeds in this call for candidates. The law is accompanied by reports of an increase in the risk of corruption. Let me mention the provisions aimed at explicitly reducing corruption risks. The law establishes a dedicated company that will deal with the construction and management of the second track. Such a case is not the first in Slovenia, such a case is the case of the state-owned company Infra, which builds the infrastructure at the hydroelectric power plant on the lower Sava. In the parliamentary group, we estimate that such an approach to investment is more transparent than it is for a billion euro of assets to be managed by one of the state-owned enterprises already engaged in a host of other activities and businesses, and therefore it would be significantly more difficult to monitor possible malicious outflows of funds. This is also due to the potential for the participation of the LAGs which cannot be included by ownership in other companies, such as the Slovenian Railway-Infrastructure. Next. The Ministry is responsible for monitoring the capital company. The responsible minister, not Minister Gasperšič, but Minister Gasperšič, is responsible, but every time. So it is not a case of policy being able to wash its hands in one of the Halls of the National Assembly, as it is now doing in the TES 6 hearings, but it will be directly in line with criminal law and every time minister. Furthermore, in addition to the provisions of the law governing integrity and the prevention of corruption, provisions relating to the incompatibilities of the performance of the function, the prohibition of membership and activities, the prohibition and restriction of the acceptance of gifts and the restriction of business applicable to professional officials, the declaration of property status, the provisions on lobbying and the like shall apply mutatis mutandis to the administration of the company. A member of the Management Board or of the Supervisory Board may be designated as a person who, in addition to the conditions laid down by the law governing companies, fulfils the specific conditions laid down in this Act, such as integrity, business ethics, experience, business performance, adequate level of education, at least 10 years of relevant work experience and so on. Nor must the company have a business share in the property of natural persons or legal persons of private law; the company must be a contracting entity after public procurement. These are all the circumstances that significantly move this project away from TES 6, which is often mentioned. Last but not least, the role of the Court of Auditors, the Financial Administration of the Republic of Slovenia, is laid down in the law, so that we estimate that this has ensured that what everyone is alerting to will not happen. The second track project is a Slovenian project, a regional project as well as a European project, we believe that we need to move the matter from a dead point. Today, as I said before, one important step is to do so, regardless of the urgency of the law. So I don't give anyone silence or cover for the execution of corruption, fraud or other crimes, nor to those who are today skeptical of the law, but some of them even promise themselves at the same time that they will be in power at a time when this project is actually being built. It follows from all of this that we will support this proposal of the Modern Centre Party Group.

I have repeatedly stressed that I like the issues of economy, projects, infrastructure in some way when they appear on the agenda or on the committee or Parliament, because I find that we may be discussing too much in Parliament about topics that are not so important for some economic growth, the well-being of our country. I am so pleased in this respect that today, in some ways, the second track has also come into the parliamentary debate as a final solution. We had a bunch of extraordinary meetings, a placard, when we somehow called on this government, and the minister to finally move something, but today we are at this stage when we came into the cooperative phase of the project, which will have long-term positive effects for our country. Of course, at some point, I may be disappointed when in some debates we encounter the dissection of a project or even in this case of a law, not to say, with some arguments that sometimes are, I could say that someone is pulling them out of scarves. In fact, we are prisoners of some stories of Slovenia in the past, projects that may not be so honorable to us, but we must cut this bad practice and also trust ourselves in some way when we are embarking on new projects and when these projects enable us to develop Slovenia, develop infrastructure and thus the economy throughout Slovenia. As has been said, this government and also the minister have often been perceived as not doing enough to develop infrastructure. I must say that we have also repeatedly warned the Ministry of Finance that infrastructure projects are those that increase economic growth and that this initial belief of the Ministry of Finance can be reversed. In fact, today we are on this path to build projects that will have positive effects on all of us in the long run. I also remember the discussions in this Chamber, when we were sort of limiting ourselves, referring to fiscal constraints and so on. I think that the view of the economy needs to be optimistic for smooth investment cycles and that the basis of any economic development is infrastructure projects that enable this development. However, I must say that, of course, all the governments so far have made such and different efforts that have never produced success. This project has been going on for 20 years, and I don't know if they've been preparing these projects in their pocket, but they never analyzed it. Thus, the Mira Cerar government has cut off this bad practice, moved from some ideas to action, and today we are here to enable this project to be realized by this law. I think that this is an important project, including a specific project approach to this realization of this second track project, and we can say that this is an example of good teamwork practice, including the innovative approach of a team of experts who have joined together, from construction workers, economic experts and individuals who have experience in projects. Of course, I think it is with this law – someone will say why it is necessary to adopt the law. Of course, a number of bad practices from the past, which have in some way contaminated us with some pessimism, that we can no longer do anything, we are doing it with some fear, I think that it is also possible for this reason to establish in Parliament a framework within which this project will be implemented, and that it will take place in the way we all want, and that the financial structure envisaged in this law will be carried out as soon as possible. As far as the various stakeholders involved in this project are concerned, of course, I have to defend the legislation and the drafter again, I think the lawmaker, this is the Ministry, that this is a dispersed risk and a financial structure involving different stakeholders. It would be simple to say, of course, to build this, to get the money from the budget, to commit ourselves and, of course, to prevent the other many projects that are still ahead of us. So I think that this solution, which includes Luke Koper, which, of course, will have the greatest benefit, including truck operators, and then sources from European sources, is the Juncker Fund and the European Investment Bank. This is actually a diversified financial structure that will allow other infrastructure projects in Slovenia to be implemented in the future. As Slovenia has already achieved economic growth, we must not stop here, we must develop all regions in Slovenia evenly. Of course, I will also touch the third development axis here, because if the second track were to be built solely from budgetary resources, we would be in debt and would not be able to carry out other infrastructure projects that are still ahead of us. In this way, the law will also allow for a balanced development of Slovenia and will also allow the development of all the provinces in Slovenia in the same way through the construction of many other infrastructure projects, perhaps in a similar model. If I finish. Of course, I think that the law is an appropriate way to approach the implementation of this project, in order to allow the European funds already mentioned by the Juncker Fund to be drawn up, and that, in addition to allowing for the integration of the behind countries, the solution itself and also the law to be integrated in Europe. We have before us discussions on what this Europe, Europe of two speeds will be like, and I think that this project can also be one example of good practice, to join the countries that are connecting, whose goal is to become leaders in the European Union, and not to be excluded and not to associate with neighbouring countries. As I mentioned earlier, it is also true that all stakeholders, including the railway, Luka Koper, will be involved in the project, and that they will also be linked in some way to the financing of this project and that, in addition to the positive effects that this project will bring to these companies, they will also contribute to the construction of this project with their share. That's how I support this law. Once again, it is a project that is an example of good practice and that this Gordi knot is finally cut off and that the government of Mir Cerar under the leadership of Minister Gasperšić and the team has shown the wealth, power, knowledge that this project is being implemented as soon as possible and that we are also going through a similar model of infrastructure projects ahead of us. Thank you very much.

We've taken 2% out of the system. This is a key step away from the system regime we have in the Republic of Slovenia when we talk about management of public rail infrastructure. As a builder, I look at the comparison of the Brenner tunnel. This is the largest railway tunnel in Europe since 2012 and will be completed in 2025 between Italy and Austria. This is said to be in the three-quarters share of the state railway. So Austrian and so Italian. The difference of up to 100% is local margins, which are also included in this area in this investment. A consortium has been established. This consortium had no difficulty in declaring any calls, as is the case here in the basic explanation of the bill prepared by the Government, stressing first that this is a golden rule and that we cannot go beyond this criterion to set up a new society and grant it a concession. That's not true! Consider a detailed example of the construction of the Brenner tunnel. There were also stakeholders in the European funds. There is absolutely no liability of 2TKK. It will lead this investment, the funds will be provided and, following the construction of this investment from 2025, 2TKK will receive a payment from the Republic of Slovenia for its availability. Approximately 35 million a year, if we calculate this for a concession period of 35 years, this amounts to a billion 225 million. This is said to mean that the entire investment will be reimbursed in this way; in short, the revenues will be provided and 2TDK will easily pay all the shareholders in the investment. That's what the law says. However, the key question to me is whether 2TKK will also pay to the State the share that the State will pay – this investment. That's not what this marriage is about. However, it requires the State to pay 2TKK at the time of the concession a billion 225 million. There's no such task in the marriage. I would say that both sides need to be looked at, that is to say, both responsible and other positive sides for society itself. Then on with the responsibility itself. In the event that 2TKK goes bankrupt, the law states very clearly and accurately that in this case it is a payer for the buildings and infrastructure of the State. It is said that here 2TKK has no responsibility, all its obligations to misbehave, for any financial irregularities, that in the event of no outcome, the company goes to bankruptcy, takes over by the state. The law does not govern the termination of the concession relationship with 2TKK when the concession is withdrawn or cancelled. Take, for example, that 2TKK does not act economically and that the State wants to withdraw the 2TKK concession, the law does not determine how and what. Consider the example of the law on concessions to exploit the energy potential of the lower Sava. How's it arranged there? Very precise, very precise, very specific. It is known how to act in this case, how the State will treat the concessionaire. This marriage doesn't talk about it. It is also a refocused liability on the State, not on 2TKK in the event of land transfer. I'd kind of go in a row. With regard to the number of investment laboratories themselves, I can say that we have produced two so far; the first was in December 2016, which contained even more variants of possible funding and then the possibilities of public-private partnership were processed there, and then purely budgetary funding, and this proposal, which was later in a second version in January 2017, was introduced by the Government and then proposed to finance through the capital cooperation of the Länder. I can say that it was made on conservative assumptions and is, in our opinion, also realistic, which is also checked by the European institutions, which is, of course, the European Commission itself, their expert body, JASPERS and the European Investment Bank. With them, we need to prove, justify, and thus prove that these figures are credible — both from the investment value itself and from the assumptions themselves about income, remuneration and so on. If I may say any more of the numbers that Member Shircelj asked about. The economic, internal rate of return is estimated at 6.26% after this current elaboration, the net present value of project inflows by cost-benefit is estimated at EUR 164 million, the financial return on capital at 3.1%, the weighted cost of financial resources is 3.6%. While these are economic indicators, I would ask that you do not now ask me to explain them precisely, because I am not an economist myself, but these are the figures I take from the investment laboratory. Only the project company, already foreseen and described in the law, will receive a total of between €30 million and €40 million annually. These are the inflows that will serve the company to service its obligations. Practically all the dedicated resources introduced by the law will be realised as public financial benefits and will then be paid to the project company as a payment for availability also through budgets. These dedicated resources are next. An important premium on tolls in freight transport is estimated at around €13 million per year. Then there is a transshipment fee in the port of Copier, which is estimated at a good 8 million euros a year. There is also an increased use on the railway, which is estimated at EUR 4 million. So these inflows together amount to about EUR 25 million. This is an assessment that will be valid at the beginning and depends on the volume of the transhipment and is also envisaged to increase this inflow by the growth of the transhipment. In order to ensure and service this necessary financial flow of the company, it will therefore be necessary to ensure the missing part from the budget, which is in 2026, somewhere up to €10 million for that year. Then, over the years, these dedicated revenues will increase by increasing traffic and transhipment, and only the burden of the budget, which will have to be secured to pay for this availability, will be reduced. This is therefore the way this financial structure is set up. In short, most of this burden, which needs to be provided for the financing of commitments and the operation and servicing of the investment itself, will in fact be provided by the users of this infrastructure. This also reduces the impact on the budget expenditure itself, minimises the impact on the deficit itself, which is also explained in the law, to a good EUR 500 million. In doing so, it must be said that this cost arises in the years of the largest investment investments in the project, from 2000 to 2025, where the annual impact is estimated at between €100 and €120 million, amounting to around 0.3% of GDP, which is expected to be largely neutralised, if not mostly, by the fact that an increase is also expected, i.e. an increase in the gross social product to be induced by this investment itself. In short, positive effects will be able to neutralize this impact, i.e. on the budget deficit, mostly. The very impact on public debt, of course, comes from the very responsibility. The debt is estimated at poor EUR 300 million. If this commitment were to increase, which would be the result of the proposals made by some Members here today, so that most of the project would be financed from the budget – in which case, of course, a larger part of the debt – would have to be provided, in short, this impact on public debt would still be increased. Secondly, with the current funding structure, which is very carefully thought out, where we also benefit from the guarantee of this so-called Juncker Investment Fund or EFSI mechanism – in short, there will be no need to provide the State guarantee for this loan. This is also very important. I've already pointed that out on the committee, and today, I'm going to try and explain again, because I think it's important. In short, this structure of these resources now consists of capital injections, which amount to approximately 40% of the estimated value of the investment, and this capital injection therefore consists of a contribution of EUR 200 million to the Republic of Slovenia and the remaining share, in short, to the shares of the Republic of Slovenia, which will be provided by the frozen countries. Approximately 25% of the investment is from the European grants we will provide from two sources, this is from our cohesion envelope and the calls for tenders that the European Commission has already announced and we have already applied for this February, this is the so-called CEF tender, and it is very important that this call, which is now published, is ongoing and must be signed up by 14 July. This is a so-called tender that allows the combination of financial sources of investment, in English they call it a blending call. In short, this call is very important for us to combine different sources of funding; these are the capital injections of the budget, capital injections from other countries, debt, European grants and then this EFSI guarantee of the mechanism is available for these commitments. In short, this call is specially prepared for the types of projects that we set up with this law, and it can be said that it is practically written on the skin of our project. Therefore, the question is why we need such a law, why such a structure. In fact, the answer is in this — because this call, as it were said, is written on the skin of a project as well as we suggest. In short, this third part is an obligation, which then represents the missing part of the investment. It is estimated that approximately 30% will have to be ensured and 5% have already been invested in land acquisition, in the production of documentation projects. And the final result; to replace this capital outflow and with European funds, the share of the debt should be significantly increased, and we again know what the consequences are. If we go back to the consequences of servicing, all of this would then also be more expensive and more difficult to service all these obligations. There is much talk that this capital injection, provided by the Western countries, is more expensive, less favourable than Slovenia could be in debt through a bank. Here it is necessary to know that this obligation is not unlimited. Banks, in particular the European Investment Bank, limit their participation by loans to projects up to a maximum of 50 % of the value of investment investments; without land acquisition and so on. In short, it is already limited here how much of this credit someone can expect from the European Investment Bank. And as it is said, if the risks are not shared here, that there are no more investors, the bank is also more cautious and is not prepared to support projects with a high level of investment. All of this must be taken into account. These are the reasons why this structure was so. Once again, in the discussions that we will have and which are already under way with the Western countries, we will advocate that these interest rates, which will be charged for these capital injections, will be taken equally into account for Slovenia's participation and for the participation of the frozen countries. Here equality will be ensured, we will try and advocate that these amounts of interest will be as much as we can get for funds through the European Investment Bank. These are the starting points that we will follow in these discussions. Expecting that there is also any return to these other countries and that this yield is significantly higher than it would have been from any other source, of course, it does not. There is no such guaranteed return here, and the law provides, and this is also one of the important principles that capital investors, co-owners in the company bear all the risks that this company takes with regard to the construction and financing of the project. This is also a very important element. There are no guaranteed returns for anyone here. If I say any more things about value added tax. It was a matter of calculating the reverse tax liability. This is based on Article 76a of the Value Added Tax Act, which is based on Council Directive 2006/112/EC, in its Article 199. Similar or equivalent principles of this reverse tax liability calculation are also applied by Dars, so that the same principles will be applied here. About the speed of the ride. The speed of driving determines mainly the shape or heels of the second track. This route has been defined and, if you say so domestically, is sealed with the adopted State Spatial Plan. The shape, the course of this route can no longer be changed, because this would mean a change in the state spatial plan. This project speed of 160 kilometers per hour is important because of how to place the route in space. We know that freight trains on our railways and on the second track will not drive at a speed of 160 kilometers per hour. However, we must, and we are also doing so on the rest of our rail network, to ensure the requirements laid down for corridors by these standards for nuclear TNT corridors. These requirements relate, for example, to the length of trains which must be at least 750 metres, on the basis of the track load capacity which must be at least 22.5 tonnes per axis, to the speed of trains which must be at least 100 kilometres per hour, I speak for freight trains, the lines must be electrified and modern systems of signalling devices and traffic management of IRTMS and GSMR must be introduced. In this respect we are also updating the rest of the Slovenian network and these standards will also be provided on the second track itself. If I go back, there were also questions as to where this assessment came from that the second track has 60-80 million benefits at European level. This is the data from the cost-benefit analysis of the project, where this calculation shows that the annual benefit of the project is approximately €80 million, of which about 30% is at the expense of the territory of the Republic of Slovenia, while the remaining 60 million of these benefits are achieved in the area outside the Republic of Slovenia; this is the benefits resulting from the reduction of the transhipment if the cargo does not go to the port of Koper, but around Gibraltar to the North Atlantic ports. This is the cost of a lower environmental burden; it is also the cost of energy savings; all of these costs reduced by the cost of investment represent this net benefit. There was also a question of VAT accounting. As it is said, this is based on the European directive, which was then transposed by Article 76a of the Added Value Tax Act, which allows for the calculation of the entry and exit tax for construction services. Here it is possible that the entry VAT received by the company in the accounts at the time of construction will be calculated with the exit tax at the time of operation, as the project company will also issue invoices at the time of operation for its services, the availability or availability of the track. It must also be said that VAT is not a reasonable cost of financing. With this possibility of clearing it, we are not just reducing the investment value itself; we are reducing the need to finance VAT, thus reducing the cost to be financed by the project. There was also a speech that the Commission objected to a public-private partnership with a document. I do not know where this document comes from, but I can say that the entire Juncker mechanism and this investment programme launched by the European Commission is based on the fact that investment projects are activated in addition to budgetary resources and, in addition to European grants, to a greater extent the capital of private investors. Here, the European Commission has taken an important step to include private capital in all these financing mechanisms; therefore, I do not understand where this claim that the Commission opposes public-private partnerships is. I would only mention this because Member Han has commented that he expects me to keep on the watch and worry about the 200 million that will be invested in this project. No doubt, we'll all be taking care of it here. These are the mechanisms of the supervisory institutions. It was already mentioned, the Court of Auditors, Furs and so on. I would also like to say that it is also envisaged that capital deposits will be invested in the company by Slovenia and by the behind countries at the same time. In that part of Slovenia's contribution, it is expected to be followed by deposits from the behind countries. This will also ensure this equality of treatment between Slovenia and the Western countries. We propose that the number of these supervisors, if so called, be maintained equally, i.e. five; but that they be represented by experts on construction issues from the areas of construction, environmental protection, space, economics and law. These candidates would be proposed by the Government and confirmed by the National Assembly. We believe that in such a cumbersome law, which represents a large number of risks, there is a guardian, such as this democratic control, really the minimum that everyone should accept. In the end, we'll stay with two scenarios. The first is that these 200 million, already in 2TKK, will simply be used and the story will be completed there; the second is that this project will continue on the basis of what is written in the law, and we will probably get to this scenario as well, the new TES 6. And we strongly oppose such laws, in addition to setting up a model that is totally unnecessary, this is a model of public-public partnership, i.e. the participation of a foreign country in this project, which ultimately means nothing but co-management of a foreign country with Slovenian strategic infrastructure; and this at some high price. We know that the law contains the maximum income, i.e. 4,5 % on this contribution that this last country would place in 2TKK and all the other benefits that are planned as a source of income for this company, from the three duties of three state-owned enterprises to the integral budget being paid in 2TKK. These are all the reasons that are still not strong enough, they do not withstand any rational judgment, nor economic at the end; and that is why we will vote against it.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-04-20-SDZ7-Redna-29.ana.u213	Thank you. Today we have listened again even this afternoon to the government's assurances that with this law the railway infrastructure will remain, I quote, “in our hands” that “the law provides control mechanisms for transparent implementation of the project”. None of this is true. Firstly, the law in Article 19 clearly provides that the owner of the second track will become a company of 2TKK for 45 years. At the same time, the government advocates that the half-owner of 2TKK will be the Republic of Hungary. So the law does not preserve the second track in Slovenian ownership, but for 45 years it is passed on to co-ownership of a foreign country. Secondly, the control mechanisms in this law are extremely flawed. The establishment of a project finance council is envisaged, which I mentioned earlier, which will have five members. Four members will be proposed by ministries and only one civil society organisation, and eventually all 5 will be confirmed by the Minister of Infrastructure alone. The project council will therefore not be an independent body, but an extended deadline for the Ministry of Infrastructure. This is as I said before, as if the management of a certain company named its own supervisory board. Of course I won't support the law. Well, that's not the only reason. The main reasons are three other reasons. Firstly, I will not support the law that provides for the most expensive method of financing. Secondly, I will not support a law that allows foreign countries to blackmail us with demands for tied business and concessions in Luka Koper. Thirdly, I will not support a law that introduces three new public benefits in order to allow a return on capital to a foreign country; and that capital that is really unnecessary, since we have it ourselves, without being in charge of [[turn off the microphone]]
